**Changed:**
- *Depth by fog* (`liveoverlay.lua`, `capture.rs`): models drawn black (`UnlitGeneric`) under white linear fog from 0 to 20,000 units, so a pixel's grey grows with its distance (fog is a standard path every normal material has). Calibration squares: A and C at 500 units (C nearer the middle of the screen), B at 4,000, D plain white. The module fits `distance = a x grey + b` from A and B and uses C to tell "along the view" fog from "along the ray" fog (then divides by the pixel's ray length); keeps the last good calibration if a model covers a square; erases the squares. Old fog settings put back afterwards. Logs: `fog depth along the view / along the ray (distance = a x grey + b)`, the squares' raw values.
- *Stamp*: never runs inside itself; for models with see-through or glowing sub-materials (`$flags` additive / translucent) only the solid sub-materials are stamped (`SetSubMaterial`, restored after).
**Tested how:** module unit tests 17 passed (new: the fog calibration picks "along the view" or "along the ray" correctly and decodes both exactly; nonsense squares give no calibration; the layers test with fog squares decodes grey 0x40 to the right distance and erases the squares; test made to grab every frame, as the game does: it was flaky when all three copy sets were busy; 8 runs in a row passed); harness PASSED (186; the fog set and restored, squares at hand-checked positions, an RPG-like view model with a glowing sub-material left out of the stamp, and a view model whose drawing re-runs its own hook is stamped once). **Not tested in game.**

## 2026-10-05 Step G1 built: Skyrim draws a test cube inside its own frame (SkyCraft's in-frame pass)

**Changed:** new `skse/src/World.cpp` / `World.h` (adapted from SkyCraft's `WorldRender.cpp`, MIT): a hook right after the call to `Main::RenderWorld` (`REL::ID(36559) + 0x85E`, patched only if the bytes there are a call to `REL::ID(107142)`). It draws a 70-unit white cube (fixed light from above) into Skyrim's HDR scene target with Skyrim's main depth buffer bound (depth test + depth write) and also writes Skyrim's motion vectors (so its TAA doesn't smear it). Camera: Skyrim's own world -> clip matrix of this frame, re-based on the camera. Every graphics setting saved and put back (SkyCraft's `StateBackup`). The cube is placed once, 300 units ahead (level) and 60 below the camera, on the first frame after a save loads, then stays put. Depth convention from the existing finished-frame probe (`Draw::DepthConvention`, new), or from the camera matrix until the probe has a result. `main.cpp` installs it and re-places the cube on load; `Probe.cpp` calls `World::LogSecond`. Garry's Mod side unchanged.
**Why:** plan step G1 (DESIGN.md §12, geometry route): prove the hook, Skyrim's depth and camera on our setup before any Garry's Mod data.
**Tested how:** plugin built without warnings (`cmake --preset default` + `cmake --build --preset release`, copied into Skyrim); both new shaders compiled offline with `d3dcompiler_47` (ok). **Not tested in game.**
**Logs to read:** `skyrim.log`: `in-frame drawing: hooked after Main::RenderWorld`, `ready (36 cube vertices)`, the targets line (scene format, HDR, depth size, "drawing here"), `matrix check` (ours should equal Skyrim's screen point, about 0.500, 0.500), `depth ... from the depth probe`, `test cube placed at ...`, and once a second `in-frame drawing: N calls, N drawn, skipped ..., ms, cube at ..., on screen at (x, y)`.
**Not sure:** whether Skyrim's TAA or its depth pre-pass treats the cube right (it's drawn after the scene, as SkyCraft does); whether the HDR brightness of 1.0 looks very bright or dull after Skyrim's tone mapping (only a test colour).

## 2026-10-05 Step G2 built: the render block (Garry's Mod -> Skyrim models, textures, frames)

**Changed:** new shared memory block `Local\GmodSkyrim_render_v1` (layout in `protocol/gmsky_protocol.h` "Render block", Rust copy `protocol/src/render.rs`; DESIGN.md "G2 in detail"): a 64 MB byte ring like SkyCraft's render ring (MIT) with messages RESET, TEXTURE (BC1/BC2/BC3/RGBA8, one mip), MODEL (parts, vertices with 4 bones / weights, indices), FORGET, FRAME (objects + 3x4 matrices relative to the anchor). A Skyrim that attaches skips the unread stream and adds 1 to `reader_epoch`; Garry's Mod's `Writer::begin` then sends RESET and everything again. C++ reader, message checks and a C++ copy of the writer in the header (`gmsky::rnd`). Skyrim: new `skse/src/Render.cpp` / `.h` opens the block (every 2 s until it works), drains it every frame from the in-frame hook (at most 48 MB), checks every message, keeps models / textures / the newest frame on the CPU, logs once a second (`render block: ...`). New tool `fake_render` (tools/fakes): a textured 2-bone bar that spins and bends, 60 frames a second. `protocol_test --read-live N`: the C++ reader on the real block.
**Why:** plan step G2 (geometry route).
**Tested how:** Rust unit tests 12 passed (4 new: every message round trip + broken ones caught; wrap-around in order and whole, full ring refuses; a write that fits but would overwrite unread data after the wrap is refused; late reader gets RESET and everything again, also after a second Skyrim). Shown to fail: with the fill rule broken (pad ignored), the new overwrite test fails (the older wrap test alone did not catch it, which is why the overwrite test was added); the same mutation in the C++ copy makes `protocol_test` fail (2 failures). `protocol_test`: PROTOCOL TEST PASSED (layout numbers equal Rust's; C++ reader on C++-written messages, wrap-around, overwrite case, garbage skipped). Live, no games running: `fake_render --seconds 10`, then `protocol_test --read-live 5` 1.5 s later: READ LIVE PASSED (RESET answering the late reader, texture 64x64 RGBA8 first pixel FF 8C 00 FF, model 48 vertices / 24 triangles / 2 bones, 304 frames in 5 s in order, 0 bad). Skyrim plugin built without warnings.
**Still broken / not tested:** Skyrim's `Render.cpp` glue (opening, attaching, counting, logging) is **not tested in game**; its reading and checks are the same header code tested above. Nothing is drawn from the block yet (G3).

## 2026-10-05 Steps G5a + G6 built (bundled, user's choice): Skyrim's light; held objects follow Skyrim's camera; per-object motion vectors

**Changed:**
- *G5a* (`skse/src/World.cpp`, SkyCraft's `GatherLighting` / `Lighting`, MIT, HDR path): every frame Skyrim's directional light (sun / moon / interior: direction, colour x fade), directional ambient (weather's six colours outside, the cell's or its template's inside), the 16 point lights nearest to reaching the camera, and fog (near, far, power, clamp, colours); lit = ambient(n) + sun x max(0, n.l) + point lights with (1 - (d/r)^2) falloff; fog mixed in; `$selfillum` parts unlit. Skyrim's normals target written too (SkyCraft's `EncodeSkyrimNormal`). `$translucent` (alpha blend) and `$additive` (added) parts in a second pass, depth tested but not written. Back faces (no culling yet) are lit from the viewer's side. Logged on a cell change and every 30 s (`lighting (...)`); point lights used per second.
- *G6*: render block **version 2** (`gmsky_protocol.h`, `render.rs`): FRAME carries Garry's Mod's camera (eye relative to the anchor, pitch / yaw / roll; flag `FRAME_VIEW`), each object its entity number and `OBJ_HELD`. `render.lua` (server half, included by `server.lua`): physgun / gravity gun / +use pick-ups and drops set `NW2Bool "gmsky_held"`; client half sends the entity number, the held flag (`gmsky_render_held 1`, new) and `render.GetViewSetup()`'s view. Module `render_out.rs` / `gmsky.RenderFrame` take the view and subtract the anchor. Skyrim: a held object is moved by `M x (p - e)`, M = Skyrim camera axes x Garry's Mod view axes^T, e = Garry's Mod's eye relative to Skyrim's camera: the same place in front of Skyrim's camera of this frame as in front of Garry's Mod's camera (SkyCraft's rule for the player's body). Motion vectors: Skyrim keeps each entity's matrices from its last frame (second buffer), so TAA sees objects' own motion; held objects have none on screen. Log once a second: `held objects: ... vs Skyrim's camera (largest axis angle) ... deg, units, frame age`.
- `fake_render`, harness, unit tests updated to version 2.
**Why:** tests 79-80: "models are dark, just a bit, and there is some slight desync when dragging them around with the physgun".

Plugin built without warnings; shaders compile offline. The held correction worked by hand in Python with the same formula: same view -> unchanged; Skyrim turned 10 degrees more -> the prop turns with it (98.481, 17.365, 0); Garry's Mod's eye 5 behind -> the prop 100 ahead of Skyrim's camera. **Not tested in game.**

Garry's Mod's view of their frame vs Skyrim's camera: **0.01 degrees** on average (max 1.90), eye 0-17.5 units apart on average (max 23.2, while walking), frames 4-6 ms old. So the camera difference this fix corrects is tiny: the lag the user sees is most likely Garry's Mod's own physgun physics (the prop chasing the beam), which this doesn't touch. No axis mirrored (largest axis angle small).
**Why "flat" (my reading, not measured):** no shadows yet (G5b: neither Skyrim's sun shadows on the objects nor theirs on the world), no normal maps (Source's `$bumpmap`), no specular / phong; the ambient here (0.75 from above) is as strong as the sun (0.77), so without those the shading has little contrast.

## 2026-10-06 Indoor shadows I1b, attempt 3: Skyrim's own lookup (from Community Shaders), checked against Skyrim's shadow mask

**Why:** user, after STATUS.md: "Just use community shaders".
**Found (read only):** Community Shaders (https://github.com/doodlum/skyrim-community-shaders, commit `dd2677fc`, cloned into the untracked `reference/CommunityShaders`) reconstructs Skyrim's shaders. `package/Shaders/Utility.hlsl` (MIT, its own `LICENSE`): drawing a parabolic map (`RENDER_SHADOWMAP_PB`) and reading it (`RENDER_SHADOWMASKDPB`). Reading: p = point through the light's transform (light-local, **with its 1.111 stretch**); lower half when p.z * 0.5 + 0.5 < 0; dir = normalize(normalize(p) + (0, 0, +-1)); uv = dir.xy / dir.z * 0.5 + 0.5, v -> 0.5 v (top) or 1 - 0.5 v (bottom); compared depth = |p| / radius. Skyrim's lighting then reads each shadow light's result from one channel of its screen shadow mask (`ShadowLightMaskSelect`; the light's `maskIndex`).
**Offline on test 92's dumps:** 35.1% within 0.01 (my best guess 22%, median 2.4%); stored depth is usually 0.007-0.02 above the formula, never far below. Likely cause (not proven): the map is drawn with straight-line blending across each triangle while the paraboloid curves, so flat walls come out a little deeper in their middles; Skyrim then has the same mismatch and covers it with a bias. So a match of depth values isn't the right test; matching Skyrim's lit / dark answer is.
**Changed:** `World.cpp`: the 96 guesses replaced by `ParabolicLookup` (Skyrim's formula, using the light's real descriptor-0 transform captured with the map, re-based on the camera) and a probe that, at the 1024 points within the light's radius, compares our lit / dark (biases 0, 0.0025, 0.005, 0.01, 0.02) with **Skyrim's own shadow mask channel for that light** (clearly lit > 0.9 / dark < 0.1 only); logs agreement per bias and the stored-minus-compared percentiles. Capture also keeps `maskIndex` and `shadowBiasScale`. Dump format `GMSKYPL2` (adds mask channel and transform), 2 dumps a run. `tools/parabolic_fit.py` reads both formats and scores this formula. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`: Community Shaders' shader licence and what was adapted.
**Tested how:** plugin built, no warnings, copied into Skyrim; shaders compile offline (`fxc`). **Not tested in game.**
**Not sure:** that Skyrim's shadow mask still holds this frame's interior light channels when we draw (after the world render); if the mask reads all 1 or all 0, the comparison can't be used.
**Next:** test 93 indoors. Agreement about 95% or more at some bias -> formula confirmed, go to I2.

## 2026-10-06 Step I2: Skyrim's indoor shadows on Garry's Mod's objects (built, not tested in game)

**Changed:** `skse/src/World.cpp`:
- `CaptureIndoorShadow` (parabolic shadow light's Render hook): every time Skyrim draws a light's map, it is copied into one of 4 slots of our own (32 MB each; all lights share one slice of Skyrim's array, so it must be copied before the next light overwrites it), with the light's transform and position; the light drawn least recently gives up its slot.
- `GatherLighting`: our point lights that have a slot (and haven't moved since their map was drawn) get 1 + the slot in `lightColor.w`; their transform, re-based on the camera, goes into the new `indoorProj[4]` (+ `indoorParams`: bias 0.005, map size) at the end of the frame constants. Lights listed both as lights and as shadow lights are now used once (counted in the log; before, such a light would have counted twice).
- Pixel shader `IndoorShadow`: Skyrim's lookup (Community Shaders' reconstruction, MIT; confirmed in test 93), 3 x 3 texels inside the right half; multiplies that light's diffuse and phong. `gmsky_render_shadow_debug 1` indoors: red = indoor shadow lights blocked, green = they reach it (by their share of the light). `gmsky_render_shadows 0` also turns these off.
- Log once a second: maps copied, slot changes, shadowed lights per frame, oldest map, lights skipped because they moved, duplicates dropped.
**Differs from Skyrim / SkyCraft:** SkyCraft has no indoor shadows. Skyrim softens with 8 rotated samples (`ShadowSampleParam`); we use 3 x 3 texels. Our objects are not in Skyrim's maps, so they can't shadow themselves or each other here (that's I3).
**Tested how:** plugin built, no warnings, copied into Skyrim; all shaders compile offline (`fxc`). **Not tested in game.**
**Not sure:** cost of the copies (2-3 lights x 32 MB, up to 180 a second in test 91; not measured, watch the frame rate); whether a light's map can be stale while it's not redrawn (test 91 saw seconds with 0 parabolic renders).
**Next:** in-game test 94.

**Changed:** `skse/src/World.cpp`:
- For each indoor shadow light with a copied Skyrim map (I2's slots), our objects are drawn into our own dual-paraboloid depth map (1024 x 1024 per slot, two 1024 x 512 halves in Skyrim's layout): `IndoorShadowVS` / `IndoorShadowPS`, Skyrim's shadow-map vertex shader as Community Shaders reconstructs it (`RENDER_SHADOWMAP_PB`, MIT): uv from the direction, depth = distance / radius, the other half clipped at the same split the lookup uses. Alpha-tested parts cut out; see-through parts don't cast.
- The lookup is now one routine (`ParaboloidLight`) for Skyrim's maps and ours.
- Our objects shadow each other and themselves from these lights (model shader; bias 0.003, pushed 3 units off the surface).
- Skyrim's darkening pass: per pixel, the light Skyrim lit it with (ambient + each light x what its own map lets through) less the share of the lights our objects block there; the indoor form of the sun's "keep". It now also runs indoors when only this is on.
- `gmsky_render_shadow_debug 1` indoors: blue added where our objects block the indoor lights. `gmsky_render_shadows 0` turns this off too.
- Log: maps drawn a frame, objects in each, darkening-pass frames. Shader text split into 3 literals (MSVC's 16 KB limit; piece 2 had reached 15 KB).
**Differs from SkyCraft:** SkyCraft has no indoor shadows. **Known limit (dual paraboloid):** a big triangle crossing the split between the two halves can be cut or bent in our map; props are small, so probably rare.
**Tested how:** plugin built, no warnings, copied into Skyrim; all eight shader entry points compile offline (`fxc`). **Not tested in game.**
**Next:** in-game test 95.

## 2026-10-06 FOV fix: Skyrim's view as wide as Garry's Mod's (built, not tested in game)

**Committed before this:** `d67bbd1` (G8, test 97), user: "Commit changes, it works fine".
**Why:** test 97, the physgun halo looked smaller than the prop. Garry's Mod's FOV 100 is the width of a 4:3 view, widened for 16:9 (half-view tangent 1.589, about 115 degrees across); Skyrim was given 100 and its `worldFOV` is the width of its actual screen (frustum right 1.1918 = tan 50 degrees, logged in tests 71-74). Skyrim drew everything narrower-angled (bigger) than Garry's Mod's picture with the halo, hands and HUD.
**Changed:** `skse/src/Camera.cpp`: `ApplyFov` converts: Skyrim's half-view tangent = tan(Garry's Mod's FOV / 2) x (screen width / height) / (4 / 3), the screen shape from Skyrim's own frustum (right / top). Logged when it changes ("CAMERA FOV: Garry's Mod's 100.0 (4:3 width) on a 1.7778 screen -> Skyrim's 115.6..."), and every second Skyrim's frustum right next to Garry's Mod's tangent (equal = the same view).
**Differs from SkyCraft:** SkyCraft's comment says Skyrim's FOV is a 4:3 width; our logged frustum says it is the screen's width.
**Expected:** Skyrim's world looks wider (as Garry's Mod's own window did); props, NPCs and the halo line up with the hands' picture.
**Tested how:** plugin built, no warnings, copied into Skyrim. **Not tested in game.**

## 2026-10-06 S1 crash experiment built (not tested in game)

**Changed:**
- `gmod/module/src/shapes.rs`: `Store::tris_in_box(lo, hi, max)`: every stored triangle touching a box, duplicates (the same triangle sent in two regions) removed, at most `max`. Test `tris_in_box_dedupes_and_limits`.

- New `gmod/lua/gmsky/physground.lua` (both sides): entity `gmsky_physground_test` and console command `gmsky_test_physground old|infmap|off`. Every 0.5 s the server builds a physics mesh (`PhysicsFromMesh`) of Skyrim's triangles within 768 x 768 x 512 units of the player's feet, at the anchor, and then removes the previous one; the client builds its copy from the same store. The player collides with it (no filter). `old` = tests 12-17's setup (commit 911fb85, same calls in the same order); `infmap` = InfMap2's chunk setup as described in DESIGN.md (mass 50000, `FVPHYSICS_CONSTRAINT_STATIC`, `FVPHYSICS_NO_SELF_COLLISIONS`, `FSOLID_FORCE_WORLD_ALIGNED`, `FL_STATICPROP`, `EFL_KEEP_ON_RECREATE_ENTITIES`) plus, as InfMap2 does, custom collision checks on the player and every physics object (undone on `off`). Logs: each build before (`physground: build N (recipe): T triangles ...; player feet ..., speed, on the ground / in the air, on <entity>...`) and after (`built N in X ms: PhysicsFromMesh=..., solid, flags, mass, game flags`); every 5 s: builds, triangles, slowest ms, `ShouldCollide` calls, what the player stood on (ticks per entity class). A re-centre rebuilds at once.
- `gmod/lua/gmsky/props.lua`: the status line counts every watched class.
**Why:** S1 of the plan: does InfMap2's setup avoid tests 12-17's `vphysics.dll` crash?

**Next:** in-game tests 101-103 (S1 runs 1-3).
## 2026-10-06 In-game tests 101-103 (S1 runs 1-2): both setups crash the same way. InfMap2's setup does NOT avoid the crash. Stopped, STATUS.md written

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-101/` and `-102/` are the same run saved twice (identical files): run 1, `old`. `build/logs-ingame-test-103/` (saved by the launcher): run 2, `infmap`. Dumps: `GarrysMod/crashes/gmod_win64_20261006_022401_1_accessviolation.mdmp` (old), `..._022524_...` (infmap), read with scratchpad `dump.py` (read-only).
**Result:**
- **Run 1, `old`:** crashed on **build 5** (about 5 s in). Builds 1-4: 3,266-3,274 triangles each, player standing still at home (feet exactly on the surface), on `gmsky_field`. Build 5: 3,281 triangles, player walking (speed 209) and standing on the previous test mesh (`on gmsky_physground_test`).
- **Run 2, `infmap`:** crashed on **build 6** (about 3.5 s of building). Builds 1-6: 3,223-3,229 triangles, player **standing still** (speed 0) at home the whole time, mostly on `gmsky_field`, 35 of 157 ticks on the test mesh. `ShouldCollide` was called (2,578 times in 2 s), so the custom collision checks were active.
- **Both dumps are tests 12-17's crash exactly:** write past a block at `vphysics.dll+0xaaff0`, `rbp=0xfffd`, `r12=0xffff`, `r13=0xfffe`, `rcx=0x2fff7` (the 16-bit list full), the same inner chain (`0x97c9b`, `0x99523`, `0x97912`, `0x977b8`, `0x96cea` ... `0xa0728`, `0x98cfc`) up to `server.dll+0x2eb909` (`CollisionRulesChanged`).
- **The Lua error the user saw:** almost certainly ours, in the "built" log line after each build: `PhysObj:GetGameFlags` doesn't exist. Not a single "built" line was written, for any build, on either side. It ran after every setup call, so it didn't change what was built and isn't the crash. **Fixed:** the line uses `HasGameFlag` for the two flags. User asked to paste the error to confirm.
**What's new compared with tests 12-17:**
1. **The player doesn't have to move.** Run 2 crashed with the player standing still (speed 0) for every build. The leading explanation since test 15 ("a new mesh under the *moving* player") is wrong or incomplete.
2. **It crashes within 5-6 builds**, much faster than before (tests 12-15 needed a whole second grid). What differs: each new mesh here is **built exactly on top of the previous one** (the same triangles in the same place: the player stood still, and the old one is removed only after the new one exists, and Garry's Mod deletes removed entities at the end of the tick) while the player touches them; ~3,200 triangles each (pieces were 512).
3. InfMap2's settings (mass, static and no-self-collision flags, static-prop flag, custom collision checks) make no difference.
**Fits every run so far (not proven):** the crash needs a new mesh created **overlapping an existing mesh** at a place where **something touches both** (the player). Test 16 (pieces never overlap; 272 builds) never crashed; the overlap experiment of 2026-10-04 (overlap, nobody touching) survived; tests 12-15 rebuilt pieces where old pieces still were, under the player. InfMap2 never overlaps two meshes in the same chunk, which may be why it doesn't crash.
**Per AGENTS.md and the plan:** two setups have failed; stopped. STATUS.md "The current problem" rewritten with this evidence and the next experiments. Waiting for the user.
**Tested how (the fix):** `gmod_harness.exe`: HARNESS PASSED.

Logged per build: older meshes destroyed first, meshes with physics before and after (must be 1 after). `docs/DESIGN.md`: the rule written above S2 (S2's chunks: a fixed lattice, never overlapping; replace = destroy first, build in the same tick).

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-107/` (the launcher had already used 104-106 for earlier unsaved runs: 104 is a copy of 103; 105 and 106 have no experiment in them). Dump `GarrysMod/crashes/gmod_win64_20261006_023432_1_accessviolation.mdmp`.
**Result:** build 1: 3,869 triangles, 13.0 ms server / 11.2 ms client, "meshes with physics on this side 1 (must be 1)", mass 50000, both InfMap2 flags on, not moving. Player standing still (speed 0) at (44, -45, -1) relative to the anchor; after build 1 it stood **on** the test mesh. Build 2 (same triangles, same place): "older meshes destroyed first: 1, meshes with physics now: **0**" on both sides, then the crash. The user: "Crash without even moving."

**What this proves:** a new mesh crashes Garry's Mod's physics **with no other mesh present**, with the player standing still on the spot. Overlap is not needed; the player moving is not needed (test 103). Every crash so far: a mesh created where the player's body touches it. Every mesh created where nothing touches it survived (hundreds in tests 12-16). But build 1 in tests 101, 103 and 107 also touched the player and survived, so "touching" isn't the whole story either.

## 2026-10-06 Ghidra on `vphysics.dll`: the crash is a full 16-bit list of collision pair records; gm_flatmap's world adds 6 records per mesh triangle

**Changed:**
- Ghidra 12.1.4 (NSA's official GitHub release `ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC_20260921.zip`, 570 MB, SHA-256 `ddac49f9...d2d4db` matching the published one) unpacked to `.tools\ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC` (untracked). Uses the installed Temurin JDK 25.
- `tools/ghidra_dump.ps1`: runs one of our Ghidra scripts headless on a DLL, read-only; Ghidra's project **and its own settings/cache/temp** go to `build\ghidra` (untracked). Ghidra's defaults are `%APPDATA%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%` and `%TEMP%`; the first manual run created empty `ghidra` folders there, which I deleted; the script now keeps everything in the project (checked: none of the three exist after a run).
- `tools/ghidra/ChainDump.java` (each address: its function, the instructions around it, callers, decompiled function), `tools/ghidra/Disasm.java` (whole function's instructions), `tools/bsp_phys.py` (a map's world collision pieces from the BSP, read-only). `.gitignore`: `!*.java` added to the whitelist. No decompiled output is in the repo.
**Why:** STATUS.md idea 1: find out what the ~65,533-entry list is. User: "Download ghidra and use it to diagnose our physics problem".
**Tested how:** `tools\ghidra_dump.ps1 -Script ChainDump -Out build\ghidra\chain.txt -Offsets 0xaaff0,0x97c9b,0x99523,0x97912,0x977b8,0x96cea,0x9736c,0xa0728,0x98cfc,0x1b687,0x41b13,0x260f5` (analysis 51 s first time); `-Script Disasm -Offsets 0xaaed0`; `python tools\bsp_phys.py <gm_flatmap.bsp> -8 2 17 543`.
**Result (from the code, and the registers of the 6 dumps):**
- `+0xaaed0` (the crashing function) adds an entry to a **sorted list whose slot numbers are 16-bit**. When the list is full it "grows" to `min(2 x size + 1, 0xfffc)`, so at 65,532 it cannot grow, yet it sets "next free slot" to 65,533 (`0xfffd`). The **next** add takes the short path (`+0xaaf10`: `ebp` = free slot = `0xfffd`, `rcx` = 3 x `0xfffd`, `r11` = slot address) and writes slot 65,533, one past the end of the block: the crash at `+0xaaff0`. This matches every dump exactly (`rbp=0xfffd`, `rcx=0x2fff7`, `r12=0xffff`, `r13=0xfffe` are constants of this path). So it is an **overflow of a list that holds more than 65,533 entries**, not random damage.
- The caller `+0x97c40` adds a pair record to the list at `object + 0xa0`, keyed by the time the two objects could next touch; `+0x99380` calls it for both objects of a record. This is very likely IVP's per-object "hull manager" (one entry for every pair record the object is part of). Naming from IVP's public design; **not 100% sure of the name**, sure of the behaviour.
- `+0x96680` (its error messages name `ivp_mindist.cxx`, Valve's IVP physics engine) makes one pair record ("mindist") **for every pair (piece of object A near B) x (piece of object B near A)**. Each triangle of a `PhysicsFromMesh` mesh is its own piece. `+0x96eb0` / `+0x98880` (object created / `CollisionRulesChanged`) choose the object pairs and call the **game's collision filter** for each before making records.
- **gm_flatmap's world is 6 pieces** (floor, ceiling, 4 walls), each with a bounding sphere ~23,000 units across that covers the whole map, so all 6 are "near" any mesh. So a mesh of T triangles makes about **6 x T** records with the world alone: 7,442 triangles -> ~44,650, two-thirds of the limit.
**What this explains:** why the number of triangles matters and 2 triangles never crash (12 records); why several meshes at once crash (the world's own list holds 6 x all meshes' triangles: test 109's first two meshes, 8,919 triangles = ~53,500, survived; adding 7,413 = ~98,000, crashed); why bigger chunks are worse.
**Compared with InfMap2 (`reference/infmap2`):** its 4 maps have the same 6 giant world pieces (`tools/bsp_phys.py`), but its chunk meshes have tens of triangles (default 3 terrain samples per 20,000-unit chunk), so 6 x T stays tiny. Ours have thousands.
**Still not explained (not sure):** the rebuild tests with Skyrim's ~3,250-3,900 triangles (101, 103, 107) and test 111's build 9 need roughly another 20,000-45,000 records from somewhere. Not known which object's list overflowed in each test, or whether records of destroyed meshes linger.
**Next:** decided with the user (see STATUS.md: tell the engine the mesh and the world never collide; and/or log the real list sizes).

## 2026-10-06 Ghidra on the collision filter (vphysics.dll + server.dll); test 113 logging (not tested in game)

**Changed:** `tools/ghidra/StringRefs.java` (strings containing a text, the functions using them, decompiled) and `tools/ghidra/FieldUsers.java` (every instruction using a given field offset, by function); `tools/ghidra_dump.ps1 -Dll` now used on `garrysmod\bin\win64\server.dll` too (analysis kept in `build\ghidra`, untracked). `gmod/lua/gmsky/physground.lua`: logging only, no behaviour change: the `ShouldCollide` hook counts **every partner** a test mesh is asked about, by class; every `step:` line and the "built" line give those counts and the mesh's collision facts (move type, solid, solid flags, collision group, physics game flags, moveable, motion, contents); before each build, the world's facts.
**Found (read-only, from the code):**
- `vphysics.dll`: `+0x96eb0` and `+0x98880` (object created / `CollisionRulesChanged`) ask the collision filter (environment `+0x30`, slot 0) for **each pair** before handing it to `+0xa06f0` (vtable `+0xfe6e8` slot `+0x28`, which makes the object-pair watcher `+0xb5dd0`, which makes the pair records `+0x96680` and adds them to both objects' lists, `+0xb61a0` -> `+0xaaed0`: the crash). The filter is skipped only when the objects' engine flag bits `0x200`/`0x400` say so. `+0x161e0` is the filter: it calls the game's `ShouldCollide` with both entities (skips pairs marked for deletion). So a "no" from the game **does** stop the records.
- `server.dll`: `SetCustomCollisionCheck` (Lua binding `+0x192e30`, found via the name table at `+0xc80de0`) sets bit `0x20` at entity `+0x14ac`. `+0xe8ed0` reads that bit on both entities and calls Lua hook number `0x99` (hook names live in `lua_shared.dll`) **only on the main thread**; otherwise "collide". Its caller `+0x3b9f80` is Source's `CCollisionEvent::ShouldCollide`; before Lua it returns early on: both objects "constraint static" (no), either entity forcing collision (yes), a pusher (move type 7) against an unmoveable object (no), either solid type none (no), collision groups / contents masks / no-collide pairs (no), player-team rules. Which of these decides (mesh, world) depends on runtime values: hence the logging.
**Tested how:** `cargo build --release`; `gmod_harness.exe`: HARNESS PASSED. **Not tested in game.**
**Next:** in-game test 113: `gmsky_test_physground rebuild flat` first (never crashed in 117 builds: the numbers without a crash), then `rebuild grid`.

Not known which. 16 builds before the crash is more than test 111 (8) and 113 (1), but one run can't tell whether that is the switch or chance. **Not sure the alt-tab mattered:** tests 103, 107 and 113 crashed standing still without one.
**Next:** decided with the user. The open question is now "whose list is full, and full of what", which is STATUS.md idea 2 (count the lists).

At that instruction `rbx` is the list and `rdi` the object it belongs to: here **object `0x21d89211160`**. - **The memory route works:** `PhysObj` userdata -> CPhysicsObject (function table `vphysics+0xecbf0`) -> IVP object at **+0x10** (function table `vphysics+0xfcf30`), which points back at **+0x100**. The free-chain count equals the `+0x1c` counter on every line.
- **The world's list: 1-2 entries for the whole run** (world collisions on). Ghidra's "~6 records per mesh triangle with the world" reading was **wrong**: the world doesn't fill up.

- Build 47, the first while walking (speed 134): **before** it, right after build 46's physics was destroyed, the player's list held 196 entries, **193 of them with one object that no entity owns any more**, very likely build 46's mesh still alive inside the engine (not proven). After `SetSolid` of build 47: the new mesh 204 entries, the player 399. Crash in `SetMass(50000)`, with no list we can see anywhere near 65,532 just before it. So the list fills **inside one call**, or belongs to an object we don't list (such as the ownerless one).

## 2026-10-06 In-game test 116 (same as 115): the overflowing list is the NEW mesh's; the "destroyed" old mesh is still alive in the engine, in the same place

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-116/`. Dump `GarrysMod/crashes/gmod_win64_20261006_151549_1_accessviolation.mdmp`: identical signature; **`rdi` (the object whose list overflowed) = `0x1cbc9331160`**.
**Tested how:** user ran `gmsky_physground_world 1`, `gmsky_test_physground rebuild grid`: "Crashed after alt tabbing" (no walking: speed 0 on every build).
**Result:**
- The engine reuses three addresses for the meshes in turn (b1/b4/b7/b10, b2/b5/b8/b11, b3/b6/b9). At the crash, `0x1cbc9331160` was **mesh b11, the mesh being built**. Its list had 220 entries (218 with the player) right before `SetMass(50000)`; the crash is inside that call, so **it gained more than 65,000 entries in one call**.
- Builds 1-10: every list at 1 entry. Between build 10 and 11 the alt-tab happened (`binds: input from Skyrim OFF`, 266 ms before build 11), and the player's physics object made 194 records with b10 (touching the ground mesh, as expected).
- Build 11 destroyed b10's physics first (`PhysicsDestroy`, "older meshes destroyed first: 1"). **After that, b10's engine object was still there:** not owned by any entity, function table and back pointer intact, its own list 196/287, still holding 194 records with the player, on every step of build 11 until the crash. Test 115 showed the same (build 46 lingering with 193 player records before build 47).
- So when `SetMass` re-checked b11, a second mesh with the same 7,442 triangles was lying in exactly the same place. Pieces of b11 near b10 times pieces of b10 near b11 can reach millions of pair records, far beyond 65,532. **Not seen directly** (the crash comes inside the call, so no line after it), but it is the only object in reach with that many pieces.
**What this explains:** "never two meshes at once" (test 107) never held inside the engine: `PhysicsDestroy` doesn't remove the engine object at once when it is touching something awake (here the player). Why walking or an alt-tab matters (the player's object wakes up and touches the mesh; standing still, it sleeps and makes no records). Why 2 triangles never crash (2 x 2 pieces). Why the world switch didn't help (the world was never involved). **Not known:** when the engine finally deletes such an object, and whether chunks (tests 109-110) crashed the same way through neighbouring meshes.
**Next:** decided with the user (STATUS.md).

## 2026-10-06 In-game test 117 (`gmsky_physground_world 1`, `rebuild grid`, never over a mesh the engine holds): NO crash in 174 builds, standing, alt-tabbing, walking

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-117/`. No new crash dump.
**Tested how:** user ran `gmsky_physground_world 1`, `gmsky_test_physground rebuild grid`, stood, alt-tabbed (6 times), walked (282 of 574 player lines moving), spawned props. "Worked just fine."
**Result:**
- **Every one of 173 `PhysicsDestroy` calls left the old mesh in the engine** ("right after PhysicsDestroy the engine STILL HOLDS it"), and every one went **exactly 7 ticks later** (94-107 ms). So deletion is always put off by 7 ticks, not only while the player touches the mesh. Each build waited ~100 ms for it; no build waited longer (no "still waiting" line).
- Largest list seen: the player's, 229 entries. No crash. Tests 101-116 crashed within 2-47 builds under the same conditions. **This confirms the cause:** a new mesh built over the old one while the engine still held it.
- **Props (user: "collided 1 time then launched themselves and phased through the world"):** expected in this experiment, not a new fault. The test mesh is a flat 768 x 768 plane at the start height (-2.2), not Skyrim's ground (the player was at z 49), and it is replaced every 0.5 s. A barrel fell through Skyrim's surface (no physics there: `FELL THROUGH`), landed on the plane, then its mesh vanished for ~7 ticks and the new one appeared through it: launched at 1,700 units/s (`PASSED THROUGH`) and fell out of the box. Props need real, lasting meshes of Skyrim's triangles: S2.
**Next:** decided with the user (STATUS.md).
## 2026-10-06 S2: Skyrim's collision as physics chunks, `gmsky_ground physics` (not tested in game)

**Changed:**
- Plan: `docs/DESIGN.md` "S2 in detail (revised after tests 101-117)".
- `gmod/module/src/shapes.rs`: every region keeps a fingerprint of its content (FNV-1a of the triangle bytes Skyrim sent); `chunk_version(r0, r1)` combines a block's (missing regions count). Unit test `chunk_version_changes_only_with_content`. `lib.rs`: `gmsky.ShapesChunkVersion(rx0, ry0, rz0, rx1, ry1, rz1)` -> arrived, total, fingerprint.
- New `gmod/lua/gmsky/chunks.lua`, entity `gmsky_chunk`: chunks of `gmsky_chunk_regions` (default 2) x 2 x 2 Skyrim regions, triangles cut at the chunk's faces, InfMap2's recipe. The server builds every chunk in Skyrim's collection window whose regions have all arrived (nearest first, 3 ms per tick, at least one), rebuilds one only when its fingerprint changes, and removes chunks more than one chunk outside the window. **No chunk is built while an earlier mesh of it is still in the engine** (`C.StillInEngine`, the test-117 check, now shared: `physground.lua` uses it too). On the server a leaving chunk's mesh is recorded and its physics destroyed before `Remove`, so a same-tick rebuild already sees it. The client builds its own copy of each networked chunk (3 ms per frame), under the same rule, and moves its physics after a re-centre. Physgun and tool gun can't touch chunks. Logs: a once-a-second line on both sides, rebuilds with their fingerprints, every departure from the engine with its time, every 5 s the biggest pair-record lists.
- `terrain.lua`: `gmsky_ground physics`: the trace field is removed; `chunks.Update` every tick; the player is held until every chunk under its feet (from 64 below to 72 above) is built or empty; a re-centre moves every chunk and its physics with the anchor (logged: largest error); a new origin removes all chunks; switching back to `shapes`/`grid` removes them. The status line shows the chunk numbers and what the player stands on. `physground.lua`'s experiment refuses to start in physics mode. `server.lua`, `client.lua`: include `chunks`.
**Why:** user: "Go ahead and work on S2". Test 117 found the crash's cause and a working rule.
**Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p gmsky-module`: 27 passed. `cargo build --release` (no warnings). `gmod_harness.exe`: HARNESS PASSED (`chunks.lua` loads on both sides; the harness runs `shapes` mode and has no physics, so chunks are **not exercised**).
**Not tested in game.** Not sure: that `SetPos` on a frozen physics mesh takes effect at once (logged); how often Skyrim's re-sent regions really change (rebuilds logged); build times of dense chunks (slowest logged); that `OnRemove` on the client sees the physics object still valid.
**Next:** in-game test 118 (`gmsky_ground physics`).
## 2026-10-06 In-game test 118 (`gmsky_ground physics`): walking and props worked; crash on fast travel (new chunks built over the old ones still in the engine)

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-118/`. Dump `GarrysMod/crashes/gmod_win64_20261006_154536_1_accessviolation.mdmp`: the same overflow (`vphysics.dll+0xaaff0`, `rbp=0xfffd`, `rdi=0x18092e34a20`); chunk builds didn't log addresses yet, so the object isn't named.
**Tested how:** user switched to `gmsky_ground physics`, walked, spawned barrels, threw a grenade, then fast-travelled to Whiterun: "CTD upon fast travelling to Whiterun". No re-centre was triggered.
**Result:**
- **Before the fast travel (~80 s of physics mode):** the player stood on chunks the whole time (`standing on Skyrim's collision (chunk ...)`, 6 seconds "nothing" = in the air). Up to 9 builds a second at the start, slowest 7.0 ms, largest chunk 1,822 triangles. 12 rebuilds (fingerprint changed). The biggest pair-record list stayed at 1. Barrels came to rest 13.5 above Skyrim's ground (asleep). One barrel jittered inside an object for a few seconds (13 "FELL" / 14 "PASSED" crossings within 1 unit of a surface, then free and resting). One was flung outside the collection window ("Skyrim's collision here not arrived") and fell to gm_flatmap's floor (S3 / P5).
- **The crash:** the new origin removed all 90 chunks at once (each logged "leaves the physics engine"); 12 ticks later the player was released on new chunks. The engine keeps a destroyed mesh ~7 ticks, and my "same chunk" check compared chunk **numbers**, which after a new origin belong to other places: new chunks were built where old meshes still lay. Same cause as test 116.
**Fixed (not tested in game):** `chunks.lua`: every destroyed chunk records its box in Garry's Mod's world; a chunk waits while **any** old mesh still in the engine overlaps its box (by more than half a unit, so neighbours sharing a face don't count), on both sides. The client also waits for an older chunk entity in the same place that still has physics (its removal may arrive after the new chunk). Every chunk build logs its engine address, so a dump can be matched. `gmod_harness.exe`: HARNESS PASSED.
**Next:** in-game test 119: the same, including a fast travel, a re-centre, and loading a save.
## 2026-10-06 In-game test 119 (`gmsky_ground physics`, place-based wait): crash while running, right after a chunk next to the player's was rebuilt. STOPPED (second failed S2 attempt)

**Logs:** `build/logs-ingame-test-119/`. Dump `GarrysMod/crashes/gmod_win64_20261006_155030_1_accessviolation.mdmp`: the same overflow (`vphysics.dll+0xaaff0`, `rbp=0xfffd`), **`rdi=0x15d06615f00` = `chunk 27 -30 -1`** (built at 9610296, 2,636 triangles, never removed: the address was still its own).
**Tested how:** user: "CTD after running far away" (no fast travel this time).
**Result:**
- 250 chunks built, 185 removed (outside the window), 4 rebuilds, 8 hold lines, no crash for ~65 s of running.
- At the crash the player was **inside `27 -30 -1`'s box, standing on it, running at 593 units/s**. The last lines: its neighbour `27 -29 -1` (1,916 triangles, the next chunk in the running direction, ~300 units ahead) changed fingerprint and was rebuilt: old mesh destroyed and its entity removed at 9612687; **"gone from the engine 12 ms, 1 tick after"** (test 117, with `PhysicsDestroy` only and the entity kept, measured 7 ticks every time); the new mesh would have been built in the next tick. Then the crash, **in the neighbour's list**, not the new mesh's (test 116: the new mesh's).
- Every "gone" that came from a rebuild (entity removed) said 1 tick, in tests 118 and 119 (5 times). Tests 118's other 11 rebuilds didn't crash.
**What this suggests (not sure):**
- (a) `gmsky.PhysAlive` may report "gone" too early when the entity is removed: it checks Valve's wrapper object (CPhysicsObject) and its back pointer, and `Remove` may free the wrapper while the engine's own object (with its pair records) lingers as in test 117. Then the new `27 -29 -1` was built over the old one, as in test 116.
- (b) Or the new mesh, which is movable between `PhysicsFromMesh` and `EnableMotion(false)`, makes pair records with its dense neighbour along their shared face, and with a fast player on the neighbour that went over the limit.
- The overflow being in the neighbour's list fits neither cleanly. Not known which, or something else.
**Per AGENTS.md:** second failed attempt at a crash-free S2 (118: new origin; 119: this). Stopped; STATUS.md updated with options for the user.
